## Claude

**How ladders work.** A ladder is built inside a pit square and hangs on one of its walls, facing the shallower neighbouring square. The build rule refuses any square that hasn't been dug. You raise or lower it by clicking; colonists don't move it on their own.
- **Lowered:** your people climb down and back up, and haulers fetch what lies on the pit floor. It acts like a prison door: friendly visitors can use it, trapped enemies and animals can't, and prisoners only during a prison break.
- **Raised:** it's pulled up onto the rim and nobody can climb it.
- **No ladder and no ramp:** whatever is down there is stranded. A pit ramp is the other way out; anyone can walk it, enemies included.

The picture you saw was wrong because FOUNDRY's test-scene script placed ladders directly on the ground squares around the pit, skipping the build rule. In real play they can't sit there.

**Ladder redraw (Opus, running):** it's working from your spec. Rails stay fixed on the wall in every state, and raised means slid up out of reach with empty rails below. The wall at the top of the screen shows the whole apparatus cleanly. The wall at the bottom is barely visible when lowered and shows a ladder poking up when raised. East and west walls show angled side mounts. The current diagonal drawing is kept for a portable ladder.

**Spikes are done and installed** (`41cc8b8dcd`): a top-down view of eight stakes with their tips pointing up at you.
- Committed texture: `\\wsl.localhost\Ubuntu\home\mandrake\rm\bench\src\RimMandrake\FlowWorks\Textures\Things\Building\FlowWorks\Canals\RM_Spikes.png`
- Preview: `\\wsl.localhost\Ubuntu\home\mandrake\rm\bench\Transient\spikes\RM_Spikes_preview_truescale.png`. It's a 64 px render on flat brown only; nobody has looked at it on dirt or under water yet.
- It isn't deployed to the game yet.

I'll start a new one whenever the count would drop below three.

**How vanilla doors handle East/West** (from `Building_Door.DrawAt`/`DrawMovers` in RimSage, and the `DoorSimple_Mover` texture pulled from `resources.assets`): there is no separate side texture. A plain door slab still looks fine turned on its side; our gates are drawn from the front, so they looked like a gate lying flat.

- **Defs:** both gates now use `Graphic_Multi` (a separate picture per facing) in `FlowWorks_Doors.xml`.
- **C#:** `Building_RM_FlowDoor.DrawAt` in `RM_FlowDoors.cs` now draws the side picture upright, not turned or mirrored, when the gate runs North-South. It still slides apart like a vanilla door when it opens. North/South drawing is unchanged.
- **Art:** new side views `RM_Sluice_east.png` and `RM_SecurityGrateDoor_east.png`, made from the existing art, checked by the sprite validator and installed through `art install` with your words attached. The old pictures were copied to `_south` files; the original files stay as the menu icons.

**Which rotation is which wall** (from `RM_LadderWallMath` FX/FZ, the same convention as vanilla `TorchWallLamp`/`WallLamp`):
- **0 = north:** the far wall, top of the screen. The whole apparatus is drawn face-on.
- **1 = east:** the right-hand wall.
- **2 = south:** the near wall, bottom of the screen. Only what sticks up above the near rim shows.
- **3 = west:** the left-hand wall.

**How the art matches the spec:**
- Both states are now `Graphic_Multi`, with drawSize 1.5 and the same per-rotation offsets in both states, so the rails stay in place when the ladder moves.
- New rails were generated with codex. The ladder is the 2026-10-16 face-on master, composited onto the rails.
- **Lowered:** the ladder reaches from the floor to just above the rim. On the south wall only the hook tips show at the rim.
- **Raised:** the ladder slides up 0.38 cell with empty rails below it. On the north wall it pokes above the far rim; on the south wall it pokes up past the near rim.
- **East/west:** the side views are foreshortened and angled, with stand-off brackets running to the wall.

**Files changed:**
- The `RM_Ladder` def in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\FlowWorks\Defs\Canals\ThingDefs\FlowWorks_ThingDefs.xml`.
- 8 new textures `RM_Ladder[Raised]_{north,east,south,west}.png`, installed through the art ledger.

<result>All three findings were the staging script's fault or the intended look, not mod bugs. The game is back on `FlowWorks_OwnerReview_2026-10-18.rws`, paused at tick 17715, and nothing was saved over it.

1. **0.2 m strip (script bug).** The solver spreads water one square further each pulse (250 ticks). The script read the strip after 300 ticks, which is only one pulse. I reproduced it in an empty corner at (60..64, 100):
   - after pulse 1: three squares at 0.33 m, two dry (what the script saw)
   - after pulse 2: four squares at 0.25 m
   - after pulse 3: all five at 0.20 m, and it stays there

   The strip in the owner-review save already reads 0.20 m on all five squares. Water filling one square per pulse looks intended, so I filed no mod bug.

2. **Grey gate canals (intended look).** The canals are not dry. Each holds 0.5 m, and the water ladder draws 0.5 m with `RM_WaterThin`, whose art is grey-brown silt. The 0.5 m and 0.2 m shallows pits are grey for the same reason; 1 m is the first blue rung. Whether 0.5 m should look blue is a cosmetic call for the owner, so I added a note to `WATER_DEPTH_RESKIN_HE_LOOKS_FIRST` rather than filing a bug.

3. **Sprinklers (script bug).** The script charged the batteries with `jawa/gss_stage`, which needs the Gimme Some Slack mod. That mod isn't on the minimal list, so every call failed, and the script never checked the reply. All four batteries were empty and all four pumps unpowered, not just the Modern one. When I charged the Modern net's battery with `jawa/battery_set`, its pump drew 150 W and the head reported spraying at the next hourly pulse.

**Fixes:**
- `4421fd49b0`: `src/RimMandrake/FlowWorks/northstar/owner_review_stage.py` now waits seven pulses before reading the strip and flags it if it isn't level. It charges batteries with `jawa/battery_set` and notes any charge that fails.
- Filed and closed `OWNER_REVIEW_STAGE_FIX_WATER_STILL_ARRIVING` with the evidence; ledger commit published as `67ac4474cb`.
- No C# changed, so nothing waits on a restart or deploy.

**Still to do:** the current review save keeps its dead batteries and unpowered pumps. Re-running the fixed script, or charging the four batteries with `jawa/battery_set` and saving under a new name, would show the spray.

`infrastructure/state/BRIDGE` still names OWNER as holder; I didn't touch it.</result>

<result>Both items are built and pushed, and both are marked implemented in the ledger. Neither has been seen in the game yet: nothing was deployed because the game is in use, so a live look is owed on both (each owes an L1 check, which needs the bridge).

**A. Pipes run on to the centre (`PIPE_ENDS_BUILD_MEET_IN_THE_MIDDLE`)**
- **Before:** the pipe is our own `RM_UniversalPipe`, drawn as a vanilla linked atlas, not VE Framework. Beside a tank or machine its arm stopped at the shared edge. Hoses are ribbon meshes that ended in a brass coupling at the edge.
- **Now, pipes:** a new map layer draws the pipe's own straight piece from the edge to the centre of the building, on the pipe's own layer, below the building. On a multi-cell building the pipe goes straight in, then turns to the centre. It is rebuilt only when buildings change, not every frame.
- **Now, hoses:** a hose coupled to a tank or machine gets a matching run to the centre, from a cached mesh that is rebuilt only when the end, footprint or width changes.
- **Which buildings:** decided by the pipe network's existing rules, not a name list: liquid tanks, the pump, and anything with a pipe-adapter, converter, reactor or sprinkler component, plus bath benches.
- **Setting:** "Pipes and hoses run on beneath tanks and machines to their centre", on by default.
- Three new offline selftest cases; the FlowWorks selftest passes 602/602 and the build succeeds. The DLL was built from a clean export of the committed sources.

**B. Pump art (`PUMP_ART_RESKIN_LESS_GARDEN_HOSE`)**
- The def is `RM_LiquidPump`. Its texture is now 512×512 (the old one was 512×256, squashed): a square steel housing with a motor cap and a flanged port centred on each of the four sides. The corners and the gap between each flange and the edge are transparent.
- It is symmetric and not rotatable, so one graphic serves every facing. The description now says it takes a pipe or hose from any side. It went in through the art ledger.

<result>Wall climbing is built, pushed, and recorded as implemented in the ledger. The new climbing-gear art is installed. Nothing has been tested in game, so 4 live checks are still owed.

**How it works:** walls stay impassable for pathfinding and reachability, so the two can't disagree. A climb is a separate job: the pawn walks to the foot of the wall, climbs for about 5 s per wall square, then lands on the far side. It only works if the wall square(s) and both sides are unroofed, and the wall is at most 2 squares thick. A "wall" is any impassable thing flagged as a wall or fence, except doors and rock.
- **Colonists:** right-click a wall to get "Climb over…". It is greyed out with a reason when a roof blocks it.
- **Raiders:** anything that would break through a wall (sappers, breachers, a blocked path) climbs it instead if it wears gear. So does a raider with no way in, which re-checks every 300 ticks.
- **Settings:** an on/off switch, a speed slider, and "Raiders may climb walls" (on by default).

**Perf:** low risk. Each check reads settings and the wall's def flags before looking at apparel, and only pawns wearing gear pay any real cost. The raider no-way-in search is the one expensive check (a full path search, once every 300 ticks).

**Gaps:**
- **Not automatic for colonists.** Unlike pit walls, colonists don't climb walls on their own while doing chores; they need the right-click.
- **Raiders never spawn with the gear yet.** I didn't add it to any raider outfits, so the raider half does nothing until you decide who should carry it.

**Commits:** the DLL was built from a clean HEAD export plus my change; `.srchash` is included.

**What raiders do now.** The new behaviour runs last in the AssaultColony duty, and after the breacher and sapper steps in the Breaching and Sapper duties. It only acts when no colonist or colony building can be reached, even through walls, and a trench is what separates them.
- **Go around:** if any route exists, the game's own pathing already walks round the trench and nothing new happens.
- **Jump:** raiders with a jump pack or a leaping ability jump across and land on their feet. 10% of an industrial-or-better raid's people arrive with a jump pack on a dug map (a placeholder number), never one that would replace a shield belt.
- **Scaling ladders:** when every wall is within the portable ladder's reach (3 m), raiders set ladders of their own faction, down the near wall and up the far one. Only their faction and your colonists can climb them; shoot one (40 HP) and whoever is below is stuck in the pit.
- **Breach by filling:** otherwise they shovel in the least earth that makes a one-square path climbable.
- **Never:** sieges, trapped raiders, flyers, or raiders wearing climbing gear.
- **Settings:** a master switch, separate switches for jumps, ladders and filling, the jump-pack share, and ladder and filling speeds.

## Claude

- `Agent — Fix two-ladder trench crossing`

## Claude

Raiders vs trenches is built and published (`3f540054fa`). It hasn't run in the game yet: six live checks are owed and are in the single-run test plan. All six new code checks pass. Raiders only use the new behaviour when a trench is the only thing between them and your colony. If a way around exists, they take it.
- **Jumping:** raiders with jump packs or leaping abilities jump the trench and land on their feet. On a map with dug ground, 10% of an industrial-or-better raid's people arrive with a jump pack. That 10% is a placeholder, and a jump pack never replaces a shield belt.
- **Ladders:** when the trench walls are within 3 m, raiders set their own ladders down the near wall and up the far one. Only they and your colonists can climb them. A ladder has 40 HP, and shooting it strands whoever is below.
- **Filling:** otherwise they shovel in just enough earth to make a one-square path they can climb.
- **Unchanged:** sieges, flyers and raiders wearing climbing gear behave as before.
- **Settings:** each of the three tactics has its own switch, plus a master switch.

The build found a real problem: the game doesn't treat "down one ladder, across the floor, up the other" as a single route. So once raiders' ladders stand, they get sent down into the trench first, and your colonists can't cross a trench using two ladders either. An Opus agent is fixing that now. It's coordinating with the wall-climbing and big-creature agents, since all three change how the game plans routes. It will also move the two English-only raider messages into the translation file.

Five agents are on FlowWorks:
- the two-ladder crossing fix
- under-25 cm water look, which is also untangling its stuck commit
- wall-climb route-finding and raider gear
- the rest of the bridge family
- big creatures

FOUNDRY still has the game.

## Claude

- `Agent — Pond rung + big-creature live rows`

## Claude

Big creatures phase 1 is built and published (`6a540188a4`). It hasn't run in the game yet: seven live checks are owed. The code is in HugeThings, the mod that already holds the titan code; FlowWorks had none of it.
- **Which creatures:** only big ones are judged, starting at thrumbo size. Smaller animals pay nothing.
- **Squeezing:** a creature squeezes in a straight line through a gap at least half its width, at half speed. It scrapes built gap edges wider as it goes, and backs out if interrupted.
- **Walls:** each wall the creature reaches either holds, gets broken through, or gets smashed. A roofed wall counts its hit points 1.5× and takes ⅔ damage. Natural rock never breaks.
- **Restless meter:** it's saved with the game and shown when you inspect the creature. Each wall shows the biggest creature it can stop, and a short message pops up whenever something squeezes, breaks or smashes.
- **Settings:** every setting is in Mod Settings, with defaults matching your answers, including thrumbos breaking thin walls.
- **Tests:** 33,360 automated test cases pass, and the build has no errors.

Three gaps and risks:
- **Narrow trenches:** titans don't step over trenches narrower than a quarter of their width yet. The rule is written and tested, but connecting it needs FlowWorks' trench route code, which the two-ladder fix is changing right now. It's filed as `TITAN_TRENCHES_WIRE_MIND_THE_GAP` and I'll start it when that fix lands.
- **Squeeze looks:** it moves the body one square at a time, so it will look jumpy. Saving the game in the middle of a squeeze is untested.
- **Corner walls:** a creature may count a corner wall its route only brushes past as a wall to cross.

A new agent is writing the live test for big creatures in the same style as the FlowWorks single-run plan. It's working out whether it can share our one FlowWorks load or needs its own mod list. It's also making a pond that dries below 25 cm use the new damp-film look.

<result>I filed and fixed **`TRENCH_CROSSING_FIX_DOWN_ACROSS_AND_UP`**. A trench with a usable ladder on each wall can now be crossed bank to bank in one route. I built and tested it offline only; nothing has been run in the game yet.

**Mechanism:** `RM_PocketGridMath.ReachVetoed` blocked reachability between any two separate ground-level areas, ladders or not. Ladders only counted as a way in or out of a pit at the start or end of the trip. The pathfinder could already walk down, across and up, but reachability said no. So raiders were sent into the trench first, and colonists crossing by two ladders were refused.

**Fix:** reachability now treats each usable ladder as a link between the two areas on either side of its wall. Who may use a ladder is unchanged: the prison-door rule, a raid's own ladders, and raised ladders still block. If the ladders join the two banks, the veto is lifted. The map-edge check works the same way, so a ringed island with ladders out is no longer cut off. This only loosens the veto; the pathfinder is untouched. The raider code's fallback and comment are updated, and its two English messages are now Keyed strings (the keys file had no peer edits).

**Selftests:** a new case in `CliffMaskChecks.cs` covers the colonist crossing, a raider on its own ladders, a hostile refused, a raised ladder blocking, one ladder not being a crossing, the pathfinder agreeing, and the laddered island. All 624 FlowWorks checks pass.

**Published:**
- c2c7302a52: source.
- b466a50bed: DLL rebuilt from a clean checkout. The wall-climb peer's 52e55c61d1 shipped a stale DLL; this rebuild replaces it and the source check now matches.
- 5508fc144d: ledger.

Peer notes are left on both peer items.

**Live rows owed (FOUNDRY):** A2, a colonist crosses by two ladders without stalling; A3, a raid crosses straight over on its own ladders.</result>

## Claude

- `Agent — Wire titan trench step-over`

## Claude

Colonists and raiders can now cross a trench by going down one ladder, across the floor and up another, as a single trip (`c2c7302a52`). It's only tested offline so far; all 624 FlowWorks code checks pass.

- **The cause:** the game's "can I get there" check blocked every route between two separate ground areas, even with a ladder on each wall. The route itself was fine, but that check said no.
- **The fix:** each usable ladder now counts as a link between the two sides of its wall. The ladder rules are unchanged: raiders climb only their own faction's ladders, and a raised ladder blocks.
- **Side effect:** an island ringed by a trench, with ladders out, is no longer treated as cut off from the map edge.
- **Messages:** the raider messages can now be translated.
- **A bad file, fixed:** the wall-climbing agent had published an out-of-date mod code file. This fix replaced it with one rebuilt from the current code.

With that in, an Opus agent has started making titans step over trenches narrower than a quarter of their width. It has to join the big-creature code in HugeThings to FlowWorks' trench code, so it's checking how those two mods should depend on each other.

<result>The follow-up is built and pushed, but none of it has run in game yet. The code is at `52e55c61d1` and the ledger note at `0a79327f2e7e`.

**Routing.** Walls stay impassable for everyone; nothing changes in the path grid or regions. When a pawn wearing climbing gear starts a walk, the next tick runs one extra pathfind. In it, the player's climbable walls are passable but each wall square costs 20 of that pawn's steps; other buildings stay blocked. If that route climbs, the normal route is also checked. The pawn climbs only if the climb route is cheaper, it can reach the foot of the wall, and it can walk on from the landing to where it was going. Then it climbs and resumes its job, so it should not stall. The x20 factor is a setting (default 20) and also sets how long the climb takes. One limit: only player-owned walls are routed over automatically, because that is the only wall-passing mode vanilla's pathfinder offers.

**Gear.**
- **35% chance** for Wildsteam, Junkers, Blackstar, Jawa and Deep Desert raiders. Blackstar and Deep Desert are the vanilla `Pirate` and `TribeCivil` factions, relabelled. The only Jawa NPC faction is the Trade Moot (`RUT_Jawa_IndigenousTribes`). Any modded faction inheriting from vanilla `Pirate` also inherits the 35%.
- **8% chance** for the light tribal and pirate raiders (Penitent, Archer, Warrior, Hunter, Drifter, Scavenger, Thrasher).
- **100%** for sappers and breachers of any faction, given when a sapper or breach raid forms rather than at generation. Every tribal warrior can act as a sapper, so giving it at generation would have overridden the small chance.

All five factions were found. Both patches validate with 0 errors (the warnings are the intended add-if-missing pattern). The gear is never swapped for something already worn, so a pawn whose belt slot is taken goes without.

**Files:** `RM_WallClimb.cs`, `RM_WallClimbMath.cs`, `RM_WallClimbSettings.cs`, `WallClimbChecks.cs`, `FlowWorks/Patches/Pits/FlowWorks_ClimbingGearRaiders.xml` and `RimUtinni/UtinniPatches/Patches/ClimbingGear_JawaFactions.xml`.

**Tests:** the build passed from a clean copy of HEAD plus my change. All 7 wall-climb selftests pass. Two other failures in the same run: one thirst test needs a file my test copy didn't include, and one hose test missed a timing limit.

**Published:** `52e55c61d1` was stale against the commits that landed alongside it, so peers rebuilt the DLL at `b466a50bed` / `6ca8078247`.

**Perf risk:** pawns without gear pay only a cheap check. Each geared walk pays one synchronous pathfind, two when a climb is possible. That is capped at 3 per tick and not repeated for the same destination within 250 ticks. The blocked-building grid is rebuilt every 250 ticks or so.

Wall-climb route-finding and raider climbing gear are built (`52e55c61d1`). None of it has run in the game yet: five live checks are owed.

**Bugs filed, not fixed.** The fixes are not small, and the DLL rebuild would pick up peers' uncommitted FlowWorks edits.
- `WALL_CLIMB_FIX_BUDGET_CAP_LEAKY_MEMOS`: the real cap is 6 pathfinds per tick, not 3, because each plan runs two. The raider path has no per-tick cap. The memos survive loading an earlier-tick save, so they can wrongly suppress plans.
- `RAIDER_TRENCH_FIX_REACH_BOUNCE`: `ReachVetoed` in `RM_PocketGridMath.cs` is a high-severity bug. A raider on a trench floor is told the far bank is reachable, finds no path, and loops.
- `HUGE_WALLS_FIX_DECIDER_RETRIES_FOREVER`: the decider has no cooldown for an unreachable destination, and held lists are never pruned.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>`TITAN_TRENCHES_WIRE_MIND_THE_GAP` is wired and pushed. Offline selftests pass on both sides. It is now `built`, and nothing has been tried in game yet.

**How it works:** HugeThings computes each titan's stride: the widest trench it steps over, floor((B−1)/4) in cells, so 1 for breadth 5–8 and 2 for breadth 9–12. FlowWorks then treats any dug run that narrow, with undug ground on both sides, as ground. The cliff masks, the pocket grid used for reachability and the hold, the step floor, the fall detector and the walked-step cost all read this same view, so the titan doesn't fall in, whether it walks or is shoved.

**Cross-mod dependency:** neither mod references the other. HugeThings puts the stride function into one shared runtime slot named `RimMandrake.TrenchStride.v1`, and FlowWorks reads it once. If HugeThings is absent, FlowWorks behaves as before. The rule is behind a new HugeThings setting, "Titans step over narrow trenches", on by default.

**One limit:** route planning still uses the shared path grid, where a dug cell costs 300. A titan will walk around a short detour and step over only when the way round is long. It is never refused the crossing.

**Files:** new `src/RimMandrake/FlowWorks/Source/RM_TrenchStrideMath.cs`, `Superdeep/RM_TrenchStride.cs`, `SelfTest/TrenchStrideChecks.cs` and `src/RimMandrake/HugeThings/Source/Titanic/WallsSlots/TrenchStrideHook.cs`. Edited: FlowWorks `RM_PocketGrid.cs`, `RM_SuperdeepTrap.cs`, `RM_PitStepCost.cs`; HugeThings kernel, settings and language keys. Three FlowWorks pin checks were repointed to the new call sites. Both DLLs were rebuilt from a clean copy of HEAD plus my change, with `.srchash`.

**Selftests:** FlowWorks 635 ok, 0 FAIL; HugeThings fuzz all pass, including the new stride check. `selftest_hugethings_lint` fails on six giant-plant masks, and `selftest_settings_screens` fails on LeaningScrub and TheForge. I didn't change either area, but I didn't check whether they fail at HEAD too.

**PUBLISHED:** `5a98a31d4e` (code), `1dd2b3be14` (ledger).

**Live rows owed (A1–A5, need the bridge):** a breadth-5+ titan crosses a 1-wide trench; a 2-wide trench holds it, while breadth 9+ crosses; a shoved titan takes no fall while a person in the same trench still falls; setting off or HugeThings absent restores the old behaviour; the stride readout shows registered and derived.</result>

## Claude

Neither thirst failure was a bug in the mod. Both came from the test setup, so no mod code changed, only the test scripts (`58e9f914a5`). Nothing has been re-run in the game yet.

- **Salt bottle:**
  - The test checked too early. A colonist carries the bottle to a spot to sit, then drinks, and the water only counts when they finish. The test waited a fixed time and looked mid-drink. It now waits for the drink to actually finish.

- **Slime:** these checks need the biomes mod, which the short FlowWorks test list doesn't include. They passed on the 17th with it loaded. They're now marked "can't measure here" and need one run on a list that includes biomes.

The salt-bottle and sprinkler checks are already in the single-run plan.

**The bug:** the stride swap ran after `RM_CliffMask`'s and always swapped in deck-view masks, wiping out the underpass floor view. A titan under a deck planned as if decks were walkable. A titan astride a narrow trench counted as "down in a hole" and got the floor view. And stepping off that trench onto a deck put it under the deck instead of on it.

**The fix:** stride masks now exist for both views, each worked out from its own trench widths. A titan in the floor band, or down in a hole it can't step over, gets the floor view. One astride a trench it steps over keeps the deck view and steps onto decks (`StepGoesUnder` and `Entered` in `RM_SuspensionPatches.cs`). The new offline test failed before the fix and passes now. Only the live, in-game swap is still unproven.

**Minors:** all done:
- The `CanonicalStride` result is cached.
- `DeriveCell` no longer allocates per cell.
- The cliff-bits test now reads the real stride depth instead of an all-zero grid.
- When ladders can't do the crossing, raiders now breach by fill. If they're only waiting on a ladder slot, they keep waiting.
- One raider per trench square (reservation).

**Marked clean:** `RM_TrenchStride.cs`, `RM_RaiderTrench.cs`, `RM_TrenchStrideMath.cs`, `TrenchStrideChecks.cs`, `RaiderTrenchChecks.cs`.

`winbuild.py FlowWorks` and the offline selftests pass. The DLL was built from a clean HEAD copy plus my change and committed through a private index. The DLL sitting in the bench working folder is someone else's build and differs from HEAD. Rebuild before the next deploy.</result>

## Claude

The titans-under-bridges bug is fixed and its ticket is closed (`a027e060d9`). It's tested offline only; it ships in the next deploy, not tonight's session.

- **The bug:** the titan's trench step-over replaced the view the underpass uses. So a titan under a deck planned as if it could walk on the deck. A titan straddling a narrow trench counted as down in the hole. And one stepping off that trench onto a deck ended up under the deck instead of on it.
- **The fix:**
  - A titan down in the floor band, or in a hole too wide to step over, now uses the floor view.
  - A titan straddling a trench it can step over keeps the deck view, and steps onto decks rather than under them.
  - A new test failed before the fix and passes now. Only the live swap in the game is unproven.
- **Small items, all done:** raiders now fill in a trench when ladders can't get them across. Only one raider works each trench square. Two speed-ups went in as well.
- **Review:** five trench and raider-trench files are marked reviewed.

I started another review pass over the remaining unreviewed FlowWorks and HugeThings files. It skips the bridge files, which are already under review, and anything the session driver might touch.

Three agents are running: the live session driver, the bridge-family review, and this general review.

## Claude

## Claude

The bridge review found two real bugs and fixed them, published as `865b752109`. Each fix has an offline test that failed before it. They ship in the next deploy.
- **Falling from the wrong height:** when a sunken bridge collapsed, people on it fell from ground level instead of from the deck, because the deck height was read after the bridge was gone.
- **Reloading mid-collapse:** a bridge swinging down wasn't saved, so after a reload the squares that hadn't dropped yet hung in the air forever with no anchor. Older saves just load with nothing swinging.

Seven bridge code files are now marked reviewed.

Six smaller problems are filed together, and an Opus agent is fixing them now:
- **Tower rebuilds:** a tower waiting to be rebuilt still holds the bridge up.
- **Load:** what people carry doesn't count toward load, but items and bodies under the deck do.
- **Wreckage:** rebuild markers for wreckage are left hanging over the hole.
- **Running total:** one running total never resets.
- **Separate bridges:** neighbouring separate bridges draw their decks as one.

The same agent will also review what this pass didn't reach: river current and floods, the cost of fire under grates, and whether walking under a deck matches the game's "can I get there" check.

Three agents are running: the live session driver, the bridge fixes, and the three small fixes (gravship clearing, swimming toggle, setting headings).

**Leftovers fixed:**
1. Only a built tower holds a span now; a rebuild blueprint or frame does not. A rebuilt tower becomes a support again.
2. Load now counts what a pawn carries in its hands. Gear and inventory were already in the game's Mass stat, so nothing is counted twice. This shared function also feeds the pit-cover trigger, so pit covers now count carried things too.
3. Auto-rebuild blueprints left behind when buildings break on a falling deck are now cleared.
4. `BandTotal` resets for each new or loaded game, and a removed map takes its count with it.
5. Deck art links only to its own bridge's decks and anchors. I linked within the whole bridge rather than one span, so a bridge still looks continuous across a tower.
6. An item or corpse that is stuck under a deck (nowhere to slide to) is no longer counted as load; the list of these is saved.
7. The knock proof no longer moves a pawn back across maps. Two other small proof bugs are fixed: a crash on a span with no deck squares, and an item left in the pawn's hands when a drop fails.

**Review of what the last pass skipped:**
- **Grate fire cost:** cheap. The fire loop walked the live thing list while pawns could die in it; it now uses a copy.
- **Floods and river current:** no defects.
- **Underpass pathing vs reachability:** a real mismatch, too big to fix here, so I filed `UNDERPASS_REACH_FIX_DECK_VIEW_SAYS_NO`. The pathfinder can route a pawn under a deck, but the reachability check reads the deck as a wall and can refuse the trip.

## Claude

All seven bridge leftovers are fixed and their ticket is closed (`23979d509b`). They're tested offline only and ship in the next deploy.

- **Supports:** only a finished tower holds a bridge up. A tower waiting to be rebuilt no longer counts.
- **Load:** what people carry in their hands now counts. Their gear was already counted, so nothing is counted twice. Pit covers use the same count, so they now include carried items too. Items stuck under a deck no longer count toward its load.
- **Leftovers after a collapse:** rebuild markers left by things broken in a collapse are cleared, and a counter that never reset now resets each game.
- **Deck tiles:** decks join only with their own bridge. A bridge still looks continuous across its towers.

- **Floods and river current:** no problems found.
- **Liquid fire code:** now reviewed in full, so the third large file is done too.

The review found one real mismatch: someone in a hole can be routed under a deck, but the game's "can I get there" check sees the deck as a wall and can refuse the trip. An Opus agent is fixing it. Its test compares that check with the actual routes for walking under a deck, standing on one, climbing in near one, a titan beside one, and a sunken bridge.

Three agents are running: the live session driver, the under-deck fix, and the test strengthening.

<result>I fixed UNDERPASS_REACH_FIX_DECK_VIEW_SAYS_NO offline and closed it at a01c76fe25; nothing has been run in game yet.

**Mechanism:** reachability always judged on the deck view, where a deck splits the hole beneath it. The pathfinder gives a pawn in the hole the floor view, so CanReach refused walks under a deck that A* would take. A second bug sat in the pathfinder: any dug square counted as "in the hole", so a pawn on a ledge saw a sunken deck at its own height as the hole below it and could never cross.

**Fix:**
- `RM_PocketGrid.ForPawn` now gives a pawn a floor-view pocket grid whenever its path uses the floor view.
- `UsesFloorView` now picks the floor view only for a pawn standing deeper than the deepest deck the floor band passes under.
- Known limit: a ledge between two deck heights keeps the deck view, so a pawn there is refused rather than wrongly promised a walk under the shallower deck.

**Test:** new `SelfTest/UnderpassReachChecks.cs`, run from `SuspensionBridgeChecks`. It checks 1549 start–destination pairs, the reach answer against a flood of the pathfinder's moves, across your five cases. On HEAD the wiring case failed. With the fix both cases pass, and the old deck-view grid still disagrees, which shows the test can fail. Three unrelated checks failed only because my partial export lacked files they read.

The FlowWorks build passed; the DLL was built from a clean HEAD export and committed through a private index, and full-file reviews came back clean. The working-tree DLL, already stale against HEAD, is left untouched.

**PUBLISHED:** a01c76fe256bc7e1cd65af8cc90d50eaeec0a847 (fix), 3405f5e6a4d28e4f3df8d60dc54742cf1d05cc23 (ledger and review marks). The live row is filed as UNDERPASS_REACH_VERIFY_WALK_UNDER_FOR_REAL.</result>

**Class: HARNESS, two separate causes.**
1. **The 0.86–0.89 level pour is designed behaviour.** Hoses hold liquid (on by default, 5 L per cell). The first level a hose carries fills the hose out of the destination tank (`RM_LiquidNet.Credit`, `RM_LiquidNet.cs:633-640` → `FillFrom`, `:527-536`; design rev 2 §3.7 L8). Run 6 put 8,899 in the tank and 1,101 in the hose, which is exactly one level. Scene 2 got a full 10,000 because its hose was already full. The script's `_moved` counted the tank alone, so scenes 0, 3 and 4 failed (relay: 8,594).
2. **Tank → hose moving 0 is a staging gap.** `SettleTick` (`RM_LiquidNet.cs:694`) only moves tanks set to Fill or Empty (owner ruling #9, "one click on the tank"). Scene 6 left both tanks on the default, Store, so nothing could move. Its per-cycle number was also wrong: the script expected 10,000 per cycle, but a tank job moves 2,000 L (20,000 units) per cycle.
- None of the recent hose changes is involved (the pose cache, the port widen, the PIPE_ENDS stubs, the litres solver). The pump moved its full level each time.

**Fix** (`D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\FlowWorks\northstar\proof_hose.py`):
- A level now counts as arrived when the tank gained something and tank gain plus hose fill reaches one level. The script reads the hose fill from each reel (`heldUnits`).
- Scene 6 sets the source tank's job to Empty first. It passes if tank gain plus hose fill is between 20,000 and 60,000, and it reports UNMEASURED if the job can't be set.

<summary>Agent "Diagnose pit cover gives way" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The covers didn't fail. The fault was in the test harness: it treated "the colonist is on a dug cell" as "the cover gave way". I found nothing in the mod that would drop a 60 kg colonist through a 120 kg cover.

**Mechanism:** The cover rows decide a pawn fell when the depth from `ProofClimb` is above 0. That depth is the cell's dug depth, and it ignores any cover on top: `RM_NorthstarProofs.cs:185` → `RM_PocketGrid.cs:666` → `RM_MapComponent_Excavation.cs:327-335`. The read that does account for covers is `RM_SuperdeepTrap.StepDepthAt` (`RM_SuperdeepTrap.cs:149-159`). So a colonist standing on an intact cover over a 2 m cell reads depth 4, and in run 20261018T233414 the 120/220 rows recorded "gave way" at one colonist.

**Suspects cleared (read in the code, not run live):**
- **Weight sum:** `RM_SpanUtility.LoadOn` (`Building_SpanDeck.cs:229-251`) counts each pawn once. It uses the Mass stat, which already includes apparel and inventory, plus only what's in its hands, so nothing is counted twice.
- **Rating:** `CompPitCoverTrigger.RunScan` (`:53-109`) uses the lowest rating among the connected covers (40/120/220 from `PitCoverTier.cs:25-34`) times the sensitivity setting, which defaults to 1.
- **Shared span code:** the suspension-bridge load check only ever runs on bridges, never on covers.
- **`kill_hostiles`:** it kills pawns only, so it can't remove the hostile-owned covers.

**Fix:** In `live_session.py` `g_cover_tiers`, "gave way" now means the covers are gone from the map (they collapse together, read with `list_things`) and the pawn is on a dug cell. A failed read gives UNMEASURED instead of "gave way", and each step records how many covers are left. I also corrected two stale docstrings.

**Test:** I added case 8b to `selftest_live_session.py`. It uses a fake game that collapses a cover deck only when the load reaches its rating, and reports the same misleading depth 4. Before the fix it reproduced the live result exactly (40 PASS, 120/220 FAIL); after the fix all pass. Two control cases still behave correctly: a deck that gives way under any load still reads FAIL, and a failed read gives UNMEASURED. The full selftest passes, and both files are reviewed and marked CLEAN.

**PUBLISHED:** `58e64eb43670e99ad087aa65d14dc2e50a8cf128` (the fix) and `b3032b42f80abc0a9f1d67414b67aa28d724c8c9` (ledger and review marks). The item is marked implemented at `58e64eb43670` and is now `built`.

**What's left:**
- **Live re-run (owed, needs the bridge):** whether the 120/220 covers really held in run 233414 was never measured, because the harness took no reading of the covers. The re-run of the B4 cover rows answers that.
- **Unexplained:** runs 231029 and 232207 read depth 0 for some first pawns on dug cover cells, on the same DLL.
- **No C# changed,** so there was no DLL rebuild.
- **Notes:** `\\wsl.localhost\Ubuntu\home\mandrake\rm\bench\Transient\pitcover_diag\NOTES.md`. It's untracked and unpinned, so it exists only in the BENCH clone.</result>

**Which build ran:** the FlowWorks DLL in the Steam Mods folder (sha256 6947e35e…) is the one committed at `23979d509b`. It includes the linked decks, stride, and collapse-depth/swing-save fixes, but not `a01c76fe25` (underpass reach). Results came from `suspension_bridge_proof_20261019c.json`.

**Per-FAIL verdicts:**
- **MOD — the three look rows (Industrial, Modern, Futuristic):** none of the 13 suspension part defs had `CompProperties_Styleable`. Without it, setting a part's style (`RM_SuspensionPatches.cs` ~970) silently does nothing, and blueprints don't keep it either. Scrapper only passed because an unstyled part reads as the default look. I added the comp to all 13 defs; a new check in `selftest_flowworks_suspension_art.py` failed before the fix and passes after. It's a Defs-only change, so no DLL rebuild was needed.
- **HARNESS — load collapse, crush-and-wreck, shot through a solid deck:**
  - The script read injuries from a key `jawa/list_pawns` never fills. The real list only comes with `includeHealth=True`, under `health.hediffs`. This also made the grate-fire burn counts blind.
  - The crush row's "before" reading was refused, so it had no baseline.
  - In the shot row, the target stood on the anchor (solid ground), not on a deck, so no deck was between the two pawns.
- **SITE — rails-off, path across the deck, vanilla bridge refused:**
  - Rails-off: the 1-wide trench sits entirely under the 1-wide bridge, so there is no hole beside the deck to fall into (the run read `edge=False`).
  - Path: the walker went round the 1-wide trench in the same number of squares, avoiding the deck's path cost.
  - Vanilla bridge: dry dug ground isn't bridgeable to vanilla, so vanilla refused before the mod's own refusal could run.
- **FIXED-SINCE `a01c76fe25` — walk under the deck, both arrive, sunken bridge:** that commit's fix isn't in the deployed DLL.

<summary>Agent "Explain 3 suspension live oddities" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I found the cause of all three readings. One was a real mod bug, which I fixed and rebuilt the DLL for. The other two were faults in the proof script. Runs a, b and c all used the same map without `--fresh-map`, so each run started with whatever the previous runs had left behind.

1. **816 kg with one rider — MOD.** The span really was carrying about 14 pawns of roughly 58 kg each (Mass is in kg; children and teens weigh less). Run a spawned 12 riders into the bare trench, and run b built the bridge over them (13 × 58.0 = 754 kg). Each run's rider then fell in as well, giving 14 × 58.3 = 816 kg in run c. The load code itself is fine: it doesn't double-count cells, count the bridge's own parts or count the whole pawn twice. The bug was that a pawn only counts as "under the deck" once it walks there, and `Notify_DeckBuilt` (`RM_MapComponent_SuspensionBridges.cs` ~1070) never marked pawns already standing in the hole when a deck was finished over them. So they were weighed as if they were on top. A player would hit this by building a deck over a colonist working in the trench. **Fix:** a finished deck now marks the pawns on its floor as under it, and slides any items out (or marks them as under if there is no room). I added an offline check, `Suspension_deck_built_over_the_hole_floor`, which fails on the old code and passes now (FlowWorks selftest 646/646), rebuilt the DLL from HEAD plus this one file, re-reviewed it and marked it clean.
2. **Empty swing log — HARNESS.** The swing trigger works: the bridge tool's destroy goes through the anchor's own destroy, which starts the swing (`Building_SuspensionDeck.cs:299` → `Notify_SupportLost` :981). But the script called `destroy_batch` with `cells=`/`defName=`, while the tool takes `rects=`/`categories=`. The tool failed with "rects is required", so nothing was destroyed, and the script never read that reply. **Fix:** correct parameters, and the build and destroy replies are now recorded. If the anchor isn't destroyed, the row reads UNMEASURED instead of looking like a failed swing.
3. **Solid-deck pit never lit — HARNESS/SITE.** The solid deck is the control: its pit is supposed to burn, and only the people above it should stay unburnt. In run c, ignition (`RM_LiquidFire.cs:238`) found no burnable liquid in that pit, though run b lit the same cell. The exact cause was lost: the script cut the refusal text at 60 characters and threw away the chemfuel fill replies. **Fix:** the full ignite text and every fill reply are kept, and a pit that doesn't light makes the row UNMEASURED with those replies attached. The cause stays unconfirmed until the next live run.

The offline mock run and the proof script's selftest both pass. The rimflow note is on `SUSPENSION_LIVE_DIAGNOSE_FIRST_REAL_RUN`. Full notes are in `/home/mandrake/rm/bench/Transient/suspension_oddities/NOTES.md`.

**Published:** the code commit went out as `85a4a13512` (another process rebased and pushed my local `bec64eb2a6`); the ledger note is `0306439a1490`. My code-review clean marks were committed and pushed inside another window's commit `a61f357220`.

**Next:** a live re-run with `--fresh-map` on the HEAD DLL is still owed.</result>

## Claude

The three bridge oddities are explained (`85a4a13512`). One was a real mod bug, now fixed, and two were faults in the test script.

- **816 kg with one rider — real bug, fixed.** Three test runs shared one map, so about 14 pawns had piled up in the trench. A pawn only counted as "under the deck" once it walked there. So when a deck was finished over pawns already standing in the hole, they were weighed as if they were standing on top. A player would hit this by building a deck over a colonist working in the trench. A finished deck now marks pawns below as under it. A new test failed on the old code and passes now, and all 646 FlowWorks code checks pass.
- **No swing-down record — test script.** The swing works. The script called the destroy tool with the wrong argument names, so the anchor was never destroyed, and it never checked the reply. Fixed.
- **Fire pit never lit — test script, cause still open.** The script cut the game's refusal message short and threw away the fuel-fill replies, so the reason is lost. It now keeps them all, so the next run will show why.

I've told the triage agent to put two things into the pass-2 plan. The bridge test now starts on a fresh map. And a stamp check comes first, rebuilding the mod code from a clean copy if it no longer matches the code.

Three agents are running: the live driver on big creatures, the first-run failure triage, and the re-read of the three fixed files.

1. **Climb ramp rows** (`climb_proof.py`, site widened from 30×12 to 46×12). The moat row puts one Pirate on an island ringed by a sheer moat and one on the moat floor, which has a ramp out on its far side. It reads whether each can reach the map edge (`jawa/flowworks_pit_report`) plus the band components (`ProofClimb`). The moat-floor raider is the control: if it can't get out up the ramp, the row reads UNMEASURED rather than PASS. The haul row puts 10 Jade on a sheer pit floor with a Hauling-only colonist and a Jade-only stockpile (`set_work_priority`, `map_zones`, `storage_settings`). It waits 600 ticks with no ramp (the Jade must stay put), then builds a ramp and waits up to 3000 ticks for the Jade to reach the stockpile. The reload row now points at live_session B7. `CLIMB_RAMP_ROWS_BUILD_STAIRS_ARRIVED_EARLY` is closed against `608c0398f0`.
2. **B2 sea never dry**: the readout zone now sits against the east map edge at (228,15,22,16), clear of the 216-wide owner zone. The lake is painted out to the last column and that cell is read back. If the paint misses the edge or `stickyLimitlessEnabled` is off, the row is UNMEASURED SITE; an edge lake that reads as limited is FAIL MOD.
3. **B5 ice**: the vanilla 1.6 source (read through RimSage) says water only freezes at −7 °C or colder, and ColdSnap alone is only −20 °C. That is why pass 1 bottomed out at 1.4 °C and could never have frozen. The block now runs ColdSnap + DeepFreeze together (−40 °C; the game adds condition offsets), over four canals (56 squares) for up to 24,000 ticks. Ice is only judged once the canal reads −7 °C or colder. "No ice" counts as FAIL only when enough freeze chances had passed that ice was over 99% certain.
4. **B6 flood**: SeasonalFlood only starts at river banks and spreads at most 12 squares, so it could never reach the staged canal. B6 now locks `jawa/weather_set` to TorrentialRain. Vanilla's flood from that weather starts at the banks of every open freshwater body, the dug canal included. I didn't use `jawa/flowworks_spawn_flood`: it pours liquid into dug cells, which would change the very canal being judged. Floods never cover water squares, so "reached" means floodwater on the canal's banks. Afterwards the weather goes back to locked Clear, which ends the flood, and the row waits for it to recede.

**Pass 2 additions** (`design/RimMandrake/flowworks_live_pass2_2026-10-19.md`): F4 is `climb_proof.py --fresh-map`. F5 is `live_session.py --only B0,B2,B5,B6` on the same map; its dry-run printed the zones and an estimated 868 s. The total pass estimate rises to about 54 min.

**Risks for the live pass:**
- F5 holds the whole map near −19 °C for a long stretch, so colonists left outdoors may get hypothermia; heal them before any later block.
- The torrential flood covers the banks of every open freshwater body on the map, not just the canal.

Notes are in `Transient/harness_rows/NOTES.md`. I found no other claimable work.</result>

- **Mechanism:** `live_session.py` `g_cover_tiers` called `jawa/set_draft(pawnId=pid)` without `drafted=True`. `SetDraft` in `JawaBenchJobTools.cs` (~1590) changes nothing when `drafted` is left out. The trails show it: pawn depths went from 4 to 0 (back on solid ground) while `covers_left` stayed at 6. The 40 kg deck only passed because one colonist already overloads it within a single 30-tick scan.
- **Class:** HARNESS, not MOD. The mod code reads correctly:
  - `CompPitCoverTrigger.RunScan` (:61-108) sums the whole connected deck against the weakest cover's rating × sensitivity, using `&gt;=`.
  - `LoadOn` (`Building_SpanDeck.cs:229-251`) never counts a pawn on a cover as underneath, because a pit cover never lets anyone pass under it.
- **Fix (`g_cover_tiers` only):** the row now drafts with `drafted=True` and reads the draft back. Any pawn not on its deck cell when the load is read makes the row UNMEASURED instead of "held". The other helper has uncommitted edits in the same file, so I committed only my hunks, applied to HEAD, through a private index. Their working copy currently crashes in `--mock` (`zone_of_row` not defined) — that's their code, not mine.

All 4 checks failed before the fix; the whole selftest passes on HEAD plus my change. No C#, so no DLL build.

The pit covers are fine. The test was at fault again (`5c921893e8`). It never actually drafted the colonists, so they walked off the deck and the bigger covers only ever had about one pawn on them. 1. **Drafting:** the grenade and dehydration-ride rows called `set_draft` without `drafted=True`, which changes nothing, so those colonists were never drafted. They are now drafted and the draft is read back, and a pawn that leaves its cell makes the row UNMEASURED. The cover rows had the same bug and a peer already fixed them in `5c921893e8`. That commit also took in some of my early edits.

It now removes whole-body conditions like hypothermia by name and restores the Torso for wounds. B5 now heals every 2,000 ticks through the −40 °C cold and always ends the cold conditions, even after a crash. After B5 and after B6 it heals and feeds the colony and puts the locked Clear weather back. The rescue colonist is left alone.
3. **Clock and sites:** when `safe_advance` stopped early or was refused, the fallback re-ran the full tick count on top of the ticks already done; it now runs only the remainder. The script also ignored whether a site came back clear and never checked dig depths, so rows read on blocked or wrongly-dug ground were scored as mod results. Those rows now read UNMEASURED SITE.
4. **Rows that could never answer:** the dig-cost row read a work total that `flowworks_job_probe` never returns; it now reads the dig job's own cost function live. The fish row read a fish field the body report doesn't have, so it failed whenever the body was read; it now uses `jawa/fishing`. The B7 reload rows passed when a value was unread both before and after; that is now UNMEASURED.
5. **Rescue row:** the downed colonist was never checked to be actually downed, and could bleed out during the wait. It is now downed without bleeding and checked.
6. **`climb_proof.py`:** it never reset old digs before digging, and digging adds depth, so on a reused map every pit came out twice as deep. It now resets the site first and checks every dig. The ladder and the scene-E ramp are now checked too, so a missing one reads UNMEASURED instead of a mod FAIL.

## Claude

The full read of the main test script and the climbing script found 14 more test bugs, all fixed and published (`ac6f9e3fff`). Each fix has a test that fails when the fix is undone.
- **Drafting:** two more checks never actually drafted their colonists.
- **Healing:** "heal the colonists" never healed anyone, because the game tool refuses a heal that names no body part. Healing now works, and the −40 °C stretch heals every 2,000 ticks and always ends the cold.
- **Clock:** when the clock stopped early, the fallback re-ran the whole wait instead of just what was left.
- **Unchecked ground:** checks ran on blocked or wrongly dug ground without noticing.
- **Unmeasurable checks:** the dig-cost and fishing checks read fields the game never returns, and the reload check could pass on nothing.
- **Rescue:** the rescue colonist was never confirmed to be down, and could bleed out during the wait.
- **Climbing:** reusing the map doubled every pit's depth, because the script never reset old digs.

One older script still heals the broken way. Rather than patch it alone, a new agent is fixing it at the source: the game tool will heal the whole body when no body part is named and report what it removed, and every caller is being updated. That takes effect at the next game shutdown. Lesson filed (`63332a856f`).

Three agents are running: the live driver, the hose-script re-read, and the heal-tool fix.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I found the real cause, and it is not the shared settings kit. FlowWorks' settings list was spilling into a second, hidden column, and the height measured from that column shrank every frame until only "Reset all" was left.

- **Mechanism:** `src/RimMandrake/FlowWorks/Source/RimMandrakeFlowWorksMod.cs` around lines 885–890. The settings list sits in a scroll view without `maxOneColumn`, and its height for the next frame comes from `list.CurHeight`. I read the 1.6 source of `Verse.Listing` through RimSage. When rows run past the view height, it starts a new column at x = view width + 17, which is outside the scroll view, so you can't see it. `CurHeight` then measures only that last column. The live screenshot `live_session_settings_1792391946.png` agrees: the window has no scrollbar at all.
- **Fix:** added `maxOneColumn = true` to the list, with a comment saying why it must stay. `src/RimMandrake/CreatureBehaviors/Source/RM_CreatureBehaviorsMod.cs` (line ~701) had the same hidden flaw and got the same fix.
- **HugeThings is not affected.** Its screenshot `walls_slots_settings_1792394885.png` draws and scrolls fine. Its A8_dialog_group FAIL is a false alarm from the instrument: `get_ui_layout` doesn't read labels that are scrolled below the visible part of the window.
- **Test:** I added `check_scroll_listings` to `src/RimMandrake/Utils/selftest_settings_screens.py`. It models the list's column arithmetic (the bug collapses, the fix holds), scans all 2158 `.cs` files for the same shape, and checks that a planted defect is caught; all 4 rows pass. The rest of the selftest is 842/844; the 2 failures are in LeaningScrub ("no Reset-all button"), a mod I didn't touch.
- **Build and push:** both DLLs were built from a clean origin/main export plus my two edits, with 0 errors. I copied them into the clone and committed with `./publish` on named paths rather than a private index. Code PUBLISHED `5c75405998`; ledger note on FLOWWORKS_SETTINGS_MEND_EMPTY_BELOW_RESET (real cause and fix sha) PUBLISHED `b5dde2a5df`. That ledger file already had other uncommitted BENCH events in it, and they went out in the same push.
- **Not done:** I didn't mark the edited files clean, because that needs a full read of every line and I haven't done it, so they stay DIRTY. Nothing was deployed, and the live look is still owed in the next live pass.

Notes are in `/home/mandrake/rm/bench/Transient/settings_blank/NOTES.md`.</result>

## Claude

The blank settings window has a real cause and a real fix (`5c75405998`).
- **Cause:** the FlowWorks settings list overflowed into a second column the window never shows. Its height was measured from that hidden column alone and shrank every frame until only "Reset all" was left. That's why the window had no scrollbar.
- **Fix:** the list is now kept to a single column. The CreatureBehaviors settings had the same hidden flaw and got the same fix.
- **Test:** a new check catches this pattern in any mod and was shown to catch a planted case.
- **HugeThings:** it wasn't actually affected. Its "fail" came from a tool that can't read settings scrolled out of view.
- **Live look:** still owed.

I've told the pass-2 driver about the fix. It should deploy it, and at a shutdown open FlowWorks' settings once to confirm everything draws with a scrollbar. If the timing doesn't work, it notes it as owed.

A small agent is now reading the two edited settings files and the new check in full.

Three agents are running: pass 2, the sprinkler fake plus review-map script read, and this read.

     - The rimflow ledger CLI (`python3 src/RimMandrake/rimflow/cli.py`) with note, close, start, reassign, needs, implemented, verify, file, namecheck, bridge take/release/who and show.
     - Provenance flags: `--owner-said` must quote an instruction, never a question; `--by-card`; `--why`.
     - Ledger shards are append-only. A stale uncommitted shard drops upstream lines, and ledger_lint refuses the push.
     - Commits use the pathspec on the commit itself, and `./publish` pushes.
     - DLLs are built from a clean HEAD export and committed via a private index (GIT_INDEX_FILE) with their .srchash.
   - **Code review rule:** `code_review_status.py check|mark-clean|reopen`. Mark a file clean only after reading every line.
   - **zsh trap:** zsh does not word-split $VAR, so use xargs or arrays.
   - **Bridge lock:** the bridge goes to one game driver at a time. Most live "MOD" failures tonight turned out to be HARNESS misreads.
   - **Tool traps found tonight:**
     - `jawa/set_draft` with no `drafted=` did nothing. It now refuses (companion f2206548f2).

## Claude

Since the last update, three of the four helpers have finished, and the dug-water and rescue-ladder fixes are pushed. The FlowWorks mod is rebuilt with both (`ac5aa12b0d`), and both items are marked implemented. Neither fix has been tested in the live game yet.

- **Dug water never froze:** this was a gap in the base game, not in our water. When water is created during play, the game opens a water body for it but counts none of its squares as freezable. The freeze chance then works out to zero divided by zero, so the ice roll always fails, with nothing in the log. Map-made lakes are counted at load, which is why they freeze. The mod now recounts the squares just before the game rolls for ice (`95e2af617e`), with a new setting, on by default.
- **No rescue ladder:** the colonist wearing climbing gear can climb out of any pit, so that colonist counted as a rescuer, and that cancelled the ladder job. But that colonist was drafted, and the game never sends a drafted colonist on a rescue, so nobody went. <result>1. **The fault was in the test harness, not the mod.** A 100-square dug pond only gets fish about half the time. The game decides a pond's fish with a random roll that depends on its size: 0% under 75 squares, 5% at 75, 50% at 100, and certain at 200 (checked in the game source, `WaterBody.SetFishTypes` and `FishingUtility`). The FlowWorks mend already re-rolls when a pond grows past 75, 100 and 200 squares, so a 100-square pond has about a 52.5% chance of fish. Pass 2 and pass 2c ran the same build (mend shipped in f6ff944423) on the same 100-square pond: one rolled tilapia (PASS), one rolled nothing (FAIL).
2. **The empty-count problem from the freeze fix does not affect fish.** A pond's size for the fish roll comes from a counter the game adds to as each square joins (the flood fill in `WaterBodyTracker`), so ponds made during play report their size correctly. The fish population of 20 in both runs is the game's floor value.
3. **Harness change, c0b1cd39db (pushed):** in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\FlowWorks\northstar\live_session.py` the test reservoir is now 10x20 = 200 squares, where fish are certain; its fishing zone and reads moved with it.

<result>The walls harness is reworked offline and pushed. It is ready for a live check, but the live run itself hasn't happened.

- **Why the creatures went round the walls:** that was the mod working as designed. When the goal can be reached another way, a creature only breaks through if walking round costs more than 2.5 times the way through and saves at least 240 ticks. Going round a 41-tall wall with open ends costs about 2.3 times. Each lane is now ringed by a one-cell deep-water moat, which nothing can walk through, so the structure under test is the only way to the goal. When there is no other way, the creature always breaks through. Each lane layout is flood-filled before it is built and refused if it leaks.
- **Two other harness bugs fixed:**
  - Every creature's own inspect line contains "smashes up to N thick" and "squeezes gaps of N+". Pass 2 matched on that text. Its "smashed with every wall standing" rows and the canyon squeeze readings were this. The harness now matches only the job text "smashing through" / "squeezing through".
  - Walls were built with no owner, so a tame creature was allowed to break them. They are now built as the player's.
- **Mod bug, fixed:** a creature that is held at a wall records the goal as unreachable for 600 ticks. That record was not cleared when part of the wall was destroyed, so the creature reconsidered the thinner wall up to 600 ticks late. The design says it should do so at once. It is now cleared, the fuzz test has a new check, and the full fuzz passes. Commits: f6dccc290f (source), 012ec71bd7 (DLL and .srchash), 7430a26028 (item note and review marks); pushed as 55e1d92418.
- **Run time:** the 2.7 TPS in pass 2 came from about 50 bridge calls per polling step, not from the game being slow. The offline mock now needs 8,179 ticks and 3,302 calls. That gives an estimated 12 minutes live at about 60 ticks a second. It cannot fit in 15 minutes at a true 3 ticks a second: the bars themselves need roughly 8,000 ticks.
- **Watch out:** pass 2 was stopped by hand and never restored its settings, so `temperamentEnabled` is probably still False in the live config. Set it back to True before the run. A stopped run now leaves a record that the next run restores first.
- **Left:** the live run (deploy the HugeThings DLL first). Three files still need a full-file review before they can be marked clean: `WallsSlotsUtility.cs`, `RM_WallsSlotsMemoKernel.cs` and `WallsSlotsMemoFuzz.cs`. Progress notes are in `/home/mandrake/rm/bench/Transient/night/hugethings_walls.md`, and the next step is noted on the item.</result>
